def _execute_tool_sync(
        self,
        request: ToolCallRequest,
        input_type: Literal["list", "dict", "tool_calls"],
        config: RunnableConfig,
    ) -> ToolMessage | Command | list[Command | ToolMessage]:
        """Execute tool call with configured error handling.

        Args:
            request: Tool execution request.
            input_type: Input format.
            config: Runnable configuration.

        Returns:
            ToolMessage, Command, or list of Command/ToolMessage.

        Raises:
            Exception: If tool fails and handle_tool_errors is False.
        """
        call = request.tool_call
        tool = request.tool

        # Validate tool exists when we actually need to execute it
        if tool is None:
            if invalid_tool_message := self._validate_tool_call(call):
                return invalid_tool_message
            # This should never happen if validation works correctly
            msg = f"Tool {call['name']} is not registered with ToolNode"
            raise TypeError(msg)

        # Inject state, store, and runtime right before invocation
        injected_call = self._inject_tool_args(call, request.runtime, tool)
        call_args = {**injected_call, "type": "tool_call"}

        try:
            try:
                response = tool.invoke(call_args, config)
            except ValidationError as exc:
                # Filter out errors for injected arguments
                injected = self._injected_args.get(call["name"])
                filtered_errors = _filter_validation_errors(exc, injected)
                # Use original call["args"] without injected values for error 
reporting
                raise ToolInvocationError(
                    call["name"], exc, call["args"], filtered_errors
                ) from exc

            # Inside try so validation errors route through _handle_tool_errors
            return self._normalize_tool_response(
                response, request.tool_call, input_type
            )

        # GraphInterrupt is a special exception that will always be raised.
        # It can be triggered in the following scenarios,
        # Where GraphInterrupt(GraphBubbleUp) is raised from an `interrupt` 
invocation
        # most commonly:
        # (1) a GraphInterrupt is raised inside a tool
        # (2) a GraphInterrupt is raised inside a graph node for a graph called 
as a tool
        # (3) a GraphInterrupt is raised when a subgraph is interrupted inside a
graph
        #     called as a tool
        # (2 and 3 can happen in a "supervisor w/ tools" multi-agent 
architecture)
        except GraphBubbleUp:
            raise
        except Exception as e:
            # Determine which exception types are handled
            handled_types: tuple[type[Exception], ...]
            if isinstance(self._handle_tool_errors, type) and issubclass(
                self._handle_tool_errors, Exception
            ):
                handled_types = (self._handle_tool_errors,)
            elif isinstance(self._handle_tool_errors, tuple):
                handled_types = self._handle_tool_errors
            elif callable(self._handle_tool_errors) and not isinstance(
                self._handle_tool_errors, type
            ):
                handled_types = _infer_handled_types(self._handle_tool_errors)
            else:
                # default behavior is catching all exceptions
                handled_types = (Exception,)

            # Check if this error should be handled
            if not self._handle_tool_errors or not isinstance(e, handled_types):
                raise

            # Error is handled - create error ToolMessage
            content = _handle_tool_error(e, flag=self._handle_tool_errors)
            return ToolMessage(
                content=content,
                name=call["name"],
                tool_call_id=call["id"],
                status="error",
            )

    def _run_one(
        self,
        call: ToolCall,
        input_type: Literal["list", "dict", "tool_calls"],
        tool_runtime: ToolRuntime,
    ) -> ToolMessage | Command | list[Command | ToolMessage]:
        """Execute single tool call with wrap_tool_call wrapper if configured.

        Args:
            call: Tool call dict.
            input_type: Input format.
            tool_runtime: Tool runtime.

        Returns:
            ToolMessage or Command.
        """
        # Validation is deferred to _execute_tool_sync to allow interceptors
        # to short-circuit requests for unregistered tools
        tool = self.tools_by_name.get(call["name"])

        # Create the tool request with state and runtime
        tool_request = ToolCallRequest(
            tool_call=call,
            tool=tool,
            state=tool_runtime.state,
            runtime=tool_runtime,
        )

        config = tool_runtime.config

        if self._wrap_tool_call is None:
            # No wrapper - execute directly
            return self._execute_tool_sync(tool_request, input_type, config)

        # Define execute callable that can be called multiple times
        def execute(req: ToolCallRequest) -> ToolMessage | Command:
            """Execute tool with given request. Can be called multiple times."""
            return self._execute_tool_sync(req, input_type, config)

        # Call wrapper with request and execute callable
        try:
            return self._wrap_tool_call(tool_request, execute)
        except Exception as e:
            # Wrapper threw an exception
            if not self._handle_tool_errors:
                raise
            # Convert to error message
            content = _handle_tool_error(e, flag=self._handle_tool_errors)
            return ToolMessage(
                content=content,
                name=tool_request.tool_call["name"],
                tool_call_id=tool_request.tool_call["id"],
                status="error",
            )

    async def _execute_tool_async(
        self,

def _default_handle_tool_errors(e: Exception) -> str:
    """Default error handler for tool errors.

    If the tool is a tool invocation error, return its message.
    Otherwise, raise the error.
    """
    if isinstance(e, ToolInvocationError):
        return e.message
    raise e


def _handle_tool_error(
    e: Exception,
    *,
    flag: bool
    | str
    | Callable[..., str]
    | type[Exception]
    | tuple[type[Exception], ...],
) -> str:
    """Generate error message content based on exception handling configuration.

    This function centralizes error message generation logic, supporting 
different
    error handling strategies configured via the `ToolNode`'s 
`handle_tool_errors`
    parameter.

    Args:
        e: The exception that occurred during tool execution.
        flag: Configuration for how to handle the error. Can be:
            - bool: If `True`, use default error template
            - str: Use this string as the error message
            - Callable: Call this function with the exception to get error 
message
            - tuple: Not used in this context (handled by caller)

    Returns:
        A string containing the error message to include in the `ToolMessage`.

    Raises:
        ValueError: If flag is not one of the supported types.

    !!! note
        The tuple case is handled by the caller through exception type checking,
        not by this function directly.
    """
    if isinstance(flag, (bool, tuple)) or (
        isinstance(flag, type) and issubclass(flag, Exception)
    ):
        content = TOOL_CALL_ERROR_TEMPLATE.format(error=repr(e))
    elif isinstance(flag, str):
        content = flag
    elif callable(flag):
        content = flag(e)  # type: ignore [assignment, call-arg]
    else:
        msg = (
            f"Got unexpected type of `handle_tool_error`. Expected bool, str "
            f"or callable. Received: {flag}"
        )
        raise ValueError(msg)
    return content


def _infer_handled_types(handler: Callable[..., str]) -> tuple[type[Exception], 
...]:
    """Infer exception types handled by a custom error handler function.

    This function analyzes the type annotations of a custom error handler to 
determine
    which exception types it's designed to handle. This enables type-safe error 
handling
    where only specific exceptions are caught and processed by the handler.

    Args:
        handler: A callable that takes an exception and returns an error message
string.
                The first parameter (after self/cls if present) should be 
type-annotated
                with the exception type(s) to handle.

    Returns:
        A tuple of exception types that the handler can process. Returns 
(Exception,)
        if no specific type information is available for backward compatibility.

    Raises:
        ValueError: If the handler's annotation contains non-Exception types or
            if Union types contain non-Exception types.

    !!! note
        This function supports both single exception types and Union types for
        handlers that need to handle multiple exception types differently.
    """
    sig = inspect.signature(handler)
    params = list(sig.parameters.values())
    if params:

tool_node: ToolNode | None = None
    # Extract built-in provider tools (dict format) and regular tools 
(BaseTool/callables)
    built_in_tools = [t for t in tools if isinstance(t, dict)]
    regular_tools = [t for t in tools if not isinstance(t, dict)]

    # Tools that require client-side execution (must be in ToolNode)
    available_tools = middleware_tools + regular_tools

def _should_stop_others(
    done: set[F],
    *,
    handled_exception_ids: set[int] | None = None,
) -> bool:
    """Check if any task failed, if so, cancel all other tasks.
    GraphInterrupts are not considered failures."""
    for fut in done:
        if fut.cancelled():
            continue
        elif exc := fut.exception():
            if (
                id(exc) not in (handled_exception_ids or set())
                and not isinstance(exc, GraphBubbleUp)
                and fut not in SKIP_RERAISE_SET
            ):
                return True

    return False


def _exception(
    fut: concurrent.futures.Future[Any] | asyncio.Future[Any],
) -> BaseException | None:
    """Return the exception from a future, without raising CancelledError."""
    if fut.cancelled():
        if isinstance(fut, asyncio.Future):
            return asyncio.CancelledError()
        else:
            return concurrent.futures.CancelledError()
    else:
        return fut.exception()


def _panic_or_proceed(
    futs: set[concurrent.futures.Future] | set[asyncio.Future],
    *,
    timeout_exc_cls: type[Exception] = TimeoutError,
    panic: bool = True,
    handled_exception_ids: set[int] | None = None,
    handled_futures: Collection[concurrent.futures.Future[Any] | 
asyncio.Future[Any]]
    | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Cancel remaining tasks if any failed, re-raise exception if panic is 
True."""
    done: set[concurrent.futures.Future[Any] | asyncio.Future[Any]] = set()
    inflight: set[concurrent.futures.Future[Any] | asyncio.Future[Any]] = set()
    for fut in futs:
        if fut.cancelled():
            continue
        elif fut.done():
            done.add(fut)
        else:
            inflight.add(fut)
    interrupts: list[GraphInterrupt] = []
    while done:
        # if any task failed
        fut = done.pop()
        if exc := _exception(fut):
            if fut in (handled_futures or set()):
                continue
            if id(exc) in (handled_exception_ids or set()):
                continue
            # cancel all pending tasks
            while inflight:
                inflight.pop().cancel()
            # raise the exception
            if panic:
                if isinstance(exc, GraphInterrupt):
                    # collect interrupts
                    interrupts.append(exc)
                elif fut not in SKIP_RERAISE_SET:
                    raise exc
    # raise combined interrupts
    if interrupts:
        raise GraphInterrupt(tuple(i for exc in interrupts for i in 
exc.args[0]))
    if inflight:
        # if we got here means we timed out
        while inflight:
            # cancel all pending tasks
            inflight.pop().cancel()
        # raise timeout error
        raise timeout_exc_cls("Timed out")

                config,
                {
                    CONFIG_KEY_RUNTIME: runtime.patch_execution_info(
                        # node_attempt is execution count (1-indexed): 1 on

                        node_attempt=attempts + 1,
                    )
                },
            )
        try:
            # clear any writes from previous attempts
            task.writes.clear()
            # run the task
            return task.proc.invoke(task.input, config)
        except ParentCommand as exc:
            ns: str = config[CONF][CONFIG_KEY_CHECKPOINT_NS]
            cmd = exc.args[0]
            # strip task_ids from namespace for comparison (ns format: 
"node1|node2:task_id")
            if cmd.graph in (ns, recast_checkpoint_ns(ns), task.name):
                # this command is for the current graph, handle it
                for w in task.writers:
                    w.invoke(cmd, config)
                break
            elif cmd.graph == Command.PARENT:
                # this command is for the parent graph, assign it to the parent.
                exc.args = (replace(cmd, 
graph=_checkpoint_ns_for_parent_command(ns)),)
            # bubble up
            raise
        except GraphBubbleUp:
            # if interrupted, end
            raise
        except asyncio.CancelledError as exc:
            # A sync node has no asyncio context, so any ``CancelledError`` that
            # reaches here was raised by the node body itself. Surface it as a
            # regular exception so the pregel runner panics the run instead of
            # treating the task as a silent tear-down (LSD-1507).
            raise NodeCancelledError(task.name) from exc

built with `create_model` and the tool's description as its docstring. For 
pydantic v2 argument schemas I'd subclass the user's model, and for plain dict 
schemas I'd use an extra-allowing model whose JSON schema gets replaced with the
tool's own.

I'm weighing whether a hoisting step is worth the complexity, since nested 
`$defs` refs would break if the tool's schema were embedded under the tool's 
name. Pydantic-model tools are the main case, so I'm thinking about how far the 
permissive path needs to go.

I'm working out how to make nested `$ref` pointers still resolve when a 
dict-schema tool's `$defs` are placed under the tool's name. Rewriting each 
local `#/...` ref to point into `$defs/<tool>` looks like a generic fix that 
would keep the references valid.

I'm working out how to keep nested `$ref` pointers valid when a dict-schema 
tool's `$defs` are placed under the tool's name. Pydantic may rename definition 
keys on collisions, which makes a simple rewrite of local refs fragile.

I'm weighing a way to get the definition key from pydantic itself: a 
`__get_pydantic_json_schema__` hook on the generated model can read the `$ref` 
it receives and then mutate the referenced definition, so I don't have to 
predict the key.

I'm thinking about whether Pydantic's final def names can be read from the 
generated `$ref` inside `__get_pydantic_json_schema__`, so the tool's schema can
be rewritten in place without predicting the key. The idea is to take the `$ref`
from the handler, resolve it to its definition, and swap that definition's 
contents for the tool's schema with its internal refs pointed at the right 
place.

I'm checking whether pydantic's remapping step would leave nested `$ref` 
pointers broken if the def key changes, since it only rewrites refs it already 
tracks. One way to sidestep that is to have pydantic generate the final 
reference itself and point the nested refs at it.

I'm considering whether the dict schema's internal `$defs` refs could be hoisted
to the root, since embedding them under the tool's name would leave those 
pointers broken. I'm weighing whether the generator's definitions can be written
to directly, though that relies on a private API.

I'm still weighing whether nested `$ref` pointers can survive being moved under 
the tool's name, since lifting the dict schema's `$defs` to the root or 
rewriting refs both depend on knowing pydantic's final definition key. I'm 
leaning toward not relying on that key at all.

I'm considering whether to hoist the dict schema's nested `$defs` to the root, 
since pydantic's renaming of definition keys makes rewriting the refs fragile. 
I'm also weighing whether a post-processed JSON schema on a wrapping type could 
handle MCP-style tools, which often carry `$defs`.

I'm checking whether wrapping the tool's schema with `WithJsonSchema` would let 
TypeAdapter return it unchanged, so the nested `$defs` stay valid without 
depending on pydantic's key naming.

I'm settling on a post-processed schema for the decision type, where dict-schema
tools get their schema placed under their own name and their nested refs 
repointed at that location.

I'm weighing whether to post-process the whole decision schema in one place: 
dict-schema tools would get their own schema placed under their name in `$defs`,
with nested refs repointed at that spot. I'm also considering a `WithJsonSchema`
wrapper so pydantic leaves that precomputed schema alone and the refs stay 
valid.

I'm considering a uniform approach where dict-schema tools get a model named 
after the tool, and I'd like to keep nested refs valid by pointing them at the 
tool's own location in `$defs`. The open question is whether I can rely on the 
final definition key, since pydantic may rename keys on collisions. I'm leaning 
toward a precomputed schema wrapped so pydantic leaves it untouched.

I'm settling on wrapping the decision schema with `WithJsonSchema` so pydantic 
leaves the precomputed schema alone, which keeps nested refs valid without

    @functools.cached_property
    def _injected_args_keys(self) -> frozenset[str]:
        # Inspect the tool's `_run` (falling back to `_arun` for async-only
        # subclasses) for directly injected args like `ToolRuntime` or args
        # annotated with `InjectedToolArg`. These are supplied at call time
        # rather than by the model, so they must be excluded from the schema and
        # re-injected during execution. `StructuredTool` overrides this to
        # inspect its wrapped `func`/`coroutine` instead.
        for method in (self._run, self._arun):
            keys = _get_injected_args_keys_from_signature(method)
            if keys:
                return keys
        return _EMPTY_SET

    # --- Runnable ---

    @override
    def get_input_schema(self, config: RunnableConfig | None = None) -> 
TypeBaseModel:
        """The tool's input schema.

        Args:
            config: The configuration for the tool.

        Returns:
            The input schema for the tool.
        """
        if self.args_schema is not None:
            if isinstance(self.args_schema, dict):
                return super().get_input_schema(config)
            return self.args_schema
        return create_schema_from_function(self.name, self._run)

        self,
        input: str | dict[str, Any] | ToolCall,

I'm weighing whether to consume the null resume before validating it, since two

the checkpointer still has it, so I need to decide which side should win.

def _interrupt_payload(interrupt: Interrupt) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Render an interrupt for a debug event, with `response_schema` only when 
set."""
    payload = asdict(interrupt)
    if payload["response_schema"] is None:
        del payload["response_schema"]
    return payload

Reasoning:
I'm checking whether a mapped resume always has pending writes, since the 
original code only looked at it when `pending_writes` was non-empty. Interrupt 
writes from the checkpoint might cover that, but I'm not sure about 
durability="exit" or subgraph checkpoints, and there could be a case where the 
writes are empty while the resume map still has the namespace hash, which the 
original code ignores.

I'm reconsidering the change, since putting the resume-map check outside the 
`pending_writes` branch would alter behavior when no pending writes exist. I'll 
keep the original semantics and move the check back inside that branch.

exist; I'll keep that exactly.

Reasoning:
I'm checking whether the copy matters here: `task_resume_write` points at the 
pending write's list, so truncating with `del` would alter that object in place.
I'm wondering whether anything depends on the in-memory pending writes picking 
up appended values, which is the one case where copying would change behavior.

I'm weighing whether to copy the pending write's list before truncating it, 
since the `del` would otherwise mutate the in-memory object. Mutating it here 
seems harmless, since it matches what gets sent, though copying would be 
cleaner. I'm leaning toward leaving it as-is to keep the diff small, but I'm 
still checking whether the same pending write could be read by two scratchpads, 
in which case the shared aliasing would matter.

   869  
   870  def interrupt(value: Any) -> Any:
   871      """Interrupt the graph with a resumable exception from within a 
node.
   872  
   873      The `interrupt` function enables human-in-the-loop workflows by 
pausing graph
   874      execution and surfacing a value to the client. This value can 
communicate context
   875      or request input required to resume execution.
   876  
   877      In a given node, the first invocation of this function raises a 
`GraphInterrupt`
   878      exception, halting execution. The provided `value` is included with 
the exception
   879      and sent to the client executing the graph.
   880  
   881      A client resuming the graph must use the 
[`Command`][langgraph.types.Command]
   882      primitive to specify a value for the interrupt and continue 
execution.
   883      The graph resumes from the start of the node, **re-executing** all 
logic.
   884  
   885      If a node contains multiple `interrupt` calls, LangGraph matches 
resume values
   886      to interrupts based on their order in the node. This list of resume 
values
   887      is scoped to the specific task executing the node and is not shared 
across tasks.
   888  
   889      To use an `interrupt`, you must enable a checkpointer, as the 
feature relies
   890      on persisting the graph state.
   891  
   892      !!! example
   893  
   894          ```python
   895          import uuid
   896          from typing import Optional
   897          from typing_extensions import TypedDict
   898  
   899          from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
   900          from langgraph.constants import START

Reasoning:
I'm drafting the new `interrupt` signature with an optional `response_schema` 
parameter, and adding docstring text explaining it: a dict is passed through as 
JSON Schema without enforcement, while any other type is validated with 
pydantic.

Writing out the docstring now, describing the validation behavior: the schema is
surfaced, each answer is checked before it's kept, and a failed answer raises a 
validation error so it can be re-answered...

I'm checking whether the answer matches the schema: a failed answer is dropped 
along with any after it, and the resume list is sent back, though I'm unsure 
whether sending an empty list when idx is 0 is right.

I'm working out the validation path for mapped resumes: if a mapped answer is 
present, it's popped and used, and when it's None I need to keep that distinct 
from "no answer," since the current check would otherwise treat a None reply as 
missing and raise the interrupt instead of returning it.

Now I'm checking the validation-and-resume fallback for the case where the next 
value is None.

I'm settling the mapped-resume case: a `None` answer must still count as an 
answer, so I'm limiting the mapped branch to the point where the resume list 
ends, and keeping that separate from the case where nothing was supplied.

I'm making sure the mapped answer is used when it's there and that a `None` 
answer still counts as an answer, not a missing one. Then I'll validate it, 
append it to the resume list, send that back, and return it.

I'm adding a check that a `None` answer from a mapped resume still counts as an 
answer, and weighing whether to cache the TypeAdapter. Validation stays lax so 
JSON answers from HTTP validate as-is.

I'm caching the TypeAdapter is not worth it here, since building one per 
interrupt call costs only a few milliseconds for this kind of dynamic schema.

    # track interrupt index
    scratchpad = conf[CONFIG_KEY_SCRATCHPAD]
    idx = scratchpad.interrupt_counter()
    # find previous resume values
    if scratchpad.resume:
        if idx < len(scratchpad.resume):
            conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
            return scratchpad.resume[idx]
    # find current resume value
    v = scratchpad.get_null_resume(True)
    if v is not None:
        assert len(scratchpad.resume) == idx, (scratchpad.resume, idx)
        scratchpad.resume.append(v)
        conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
        return v
    # no resume value found
    raise GraphInterrupt(

            Interrupt.from_ns(

                ns=conf[CONFIG_KEY_CHECKPOINT_NS],
            ),
        )

new = '''    conf = get_config()["configurable"]
    # track interrupt index
    scratchpad = conf[CONFIG_KEY_SCRATCHPAD]
    idx = scratchpad.interrupt_counter()
    adapter = (
        None
        if response_schema is None or isinstance(response_schema, dict)
        else TypeAdapter(response_schema)
    )
    # find previous resume values
    if idx < len(scratchpad.resume):
        try:
            answer = (
                scratchpad.resume[idx]
                if adapter is None
                else adapter.validate_python(scratchpad.resume[idx])
            )
        except ValidationError:
            # stop keeping the rejected answer, and the ones after it
            del scratchpad.resume[idx:]
            conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
            raise
        conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
        return answer
    # find current resume value
    if idx == len(scratchpad.resume) and scratchpad.mapped_resume:
        v = scratchpad.mapped_resume.pop()
    else:
        v = scratchpad.get_null_resume(True)
        if v is None:
            # no resume value found
            raise GraphInterrupt(
                (
                    Interrupt.from_ns(
                        value=value,
                        ns=conf[CONFIG_KEY_CHECKPOINT_NS],
                        response_schema=(
                            adapter.json_schema()
                            if adapter is not None
                            else response_schema
                        ),
                    ),
                )
            )
        assert len(scratchpad.resume) == idx, (scratchpad.resume, idx)
    # validate before keeping, so a rejected answer leaves nothing behind
    answer = v if adapter is None else adapter.validate_python(v)
    scratchpad.resume.append(v)
    conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
    return answer
'''
assert s.count(old) == 1
s = s.replace(old, new)

23:from pydantic import TypeAdapter, ValidationError
def interrupt(
    value: Any, response_schema: dict[str, Any] | Any | None = None
) -> Any:
    """Interrupt the graph with a resumable exception from within a node.
    is scoped to the specific task executing the node and is not shared across 
tasks.

    To use an `interrupt`, you must enable a checkpointer, as the feature relies
    on persisting the graph state.

    Pass `response_schema` to declare the answers the interrupt accepts. The 
surfaced
    `Interrupt` carries it as JSON Schema. A `dict` is taken to be JSON Schema: 
it is
    surfaced unchanged and not enforced. Anything else is taken to be a type 
pydantic
    can validate: its JSON Schema is surfaced, and each answer is validated 
against it
    before it is kept. `interrupt` then returns the validated object. An answer 
that
    fails validation raises `pydantic.ValidationError` and is not kept for the 
task,
    so the interrupt can be answered again.

    Raises:
        GraphInterrupt: On the first invocation within the node, halts execution
and surfaces the provided value to the client.
        pydantic.ValidationError: If `response_schema` is a type and the answer 
does not validate against it.
    """
    from langgraph._internal._constants import (
        CONFIG_KEY_CHECKPOINT_NS,
        CONFIG_KEY_SCRATCHPAD,
        CONFIG_KEY_SEND,
        RESUME,
    )
    from langgraph.config import get_config
    from langgraph.errors import GraphInterrupt

    conf = get_config()["configurable"]
    # track interrupt index
    scratchpad = conf[CONFIG_KEY_SCRATCHPAD]
    idx = scratchpad.interrupt_counter()
    adapter = (
        None
        if response_schema is None or isinstance(response_schema, dict)
        else TypeAdapter(response_schema)
    )
    # find previous resume values
    if idx < len(scratchpad.resume):
        try:
            answer = (
                scratchpad.resume[idx]
                if adapter is None
                else adapter.validate_python(scratchpad.resume[idx])
            )
        except ValidationError:
            # stop keeping the rejected answer, and the ones after it
            del scratchpad.resume[idx:]
            conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
            raise
        conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
        return answer
    # find current resume value
    if idx == len(scratchpad.resume) and scratchpad.mapped_resume:
        v = scratchpad.mapped_resume.pop()
    else:
        v = scratchpad.get_null_resume(True)
        if v is None:
            # no resume value found
            raise GraphInterrupt(
                (
                    Interrupt.from_ns(
                        value=value,
                        ns=conf[CONFIG_KEY_CHECKPOINT_NS],
                        response_schema=(
                            adapter.json_schema()
                            if adapter is not None
                            else response_schema
                        ),
                    ),
                )
            )
        assert len(scratchpad.resume) == idx, (scratchpad.resume, idx)
    # validate before keeping, so a rejected answer leaves nothing behind
    answer = v if adapter is None else adapter.validate_python(v)
    scratchpad.resume.append(v)
    conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
    return answer


@dataclass(slots=True)

import asyncio
from typing import Literal
from pydantic import BaseModel, ValidationError
from typing_extensions import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
from langgraph.types import interrupt, Command

class S(TypedDict):
    x: list

class Answer(BaseModel):
    age: int

calls = []
def node(s):
    a = interrupt("first", response_schema=Answer)
    calls.append(("a", a))
    b = interrupt("second", response_schema={"type": "string"})
    calls.append(("b", b))
    c = interrupt("third", response_schema=Literal["yes", "no"])
    calls.append(("c", c))
    return {"x": [a.age, b, c]}

g = StateGraph(S); g.add_node("node", node); g.add_edge(START, "node")
app = g.compile(checkpointer=InMemorySaver())
cfg = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "1"}}
r = app.invoke({"x": []}, cfg); print(r["__interrupt__"])
iid = r["__interrupt__"][0].id
def attempt(ans, plain=False):
    try:
        out = app.invoke(Command(resume=ans if plain else {iid: ans}), cfg)
        print("OK", out.get("__interrupt__", out))
    except ValidationError as e:
        print("VALIDATION", e.error_count(), e.errors()[0]["loc"], 
e.errors()[0]["msg"])
    st = app.get_state(cfg)
    print("  next", st.next, [i.value for i in st.interrupts])
attempt({"age": "x"})
attempt({"age": "x"})
attempt({"age": "41"})
attempt(123)          # dict schema not enforced
attempt("maybe")
attempt("yes", plain=True)
print(calls)
print(app.get_state(cfg).values)
EOF

import asyncio
from pydantic import ValidationError
from typing_extensions import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
from langgraph.types import interrupt, Command

class S(TypedDict):
    x: list
schema = {"a": str}
async def node(s):
    a = interrupt("first", response_schema=schema["a"])
    b = interrupt("second", response_schema=int)
    return {"x": [a, b]}

g = StateGraph(S); g.add_node("node", node); g.add_edge(START, "node")
app = g.compile(checkpointer=InMemorySaver())
cfg = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "1"}}
async def main():
    r = await app.ainvoke({"x": []}, cfg); iid = r["__interrupt__"][0].id
    r = await app.ainvoke(Command(resume={iid: "abc"}), cfg); 
print(r["__interrupt__"])
    schema["a"] = int   # kept answer 'abc' no longer validates
    for ans in ["5", "7"]:
        try:
            r = await app.ainvoke(Command(resume={iid: ans}), cfg); print("OK", 
r)
        except ValidationError as e:
            print("VALIDATION", e.errors()[0]["loc"], e.errors()[0]["input"])
    r = await app.ainvoke(Command(resume={iid: "9"}), cfg); print("OK", r)
asyncio.run(main())

import json
from typing import Annotated, Any, Literal, Union
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, TypeAdapter, WrapValidator, ConfigDict, 
create_model, ValidationError
from typing_extensions import TypedDict
from langchain_core.tools import tool, StructuredTool
from langchain.agents.middleware.human_in_the_loop import ApproveDecision, 
RejectDecision, RespondDecision

@tool
def search(query: str, limit: int = 5) -> str:
    """Search the web."""
    return query

schema = search.tool_call_schema
print(schema, schema.__name__, schema.__doc__)

def keep(v, handler):
    handler(v)
    return v
Args = Annotated[schema, WrapValidator(keep)]
EditedAction = TypedDict("EditedAction", {"name": Literal["search"], "args": 
Args})
EditDecision = TypedDict("EditDecision", {"type": Literal["edit"], 
"edited_action": EditedAction})
U = Annotated[Union[ApproveDecision, EditDecision, RejectDecision, 
RespondDecision], Field(discriminator="type")]
ta = TypeAdapter(U)
print(json.dumps(ta.json_schema(), indent=1))
for d in [{"type": "edit"}, {"type": "nope"}, 
{"type":"edit","edited_action":{"name":"x","args":{"query":"a"}}},
          {"type":"edit","edited_action":{"name":"search","args":{}}},
          {"type":"edit","edited_action":{"name":"search","args":{"query":"a","e
xtra":1}}}]:
    try: print("OK", ta.validate_python(d))
    except ValidationError as e: print(e.error_count(), [(x["loc"], x["msg"]) 
for x in e.errors()])
EOF

import json
from typing import Annotated, Any, Literal, Union
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, TypeAdapter, WrapValidator, ConfigDict, 
create_model, ValidationError
from typing_extensions import TypedDict
from langchain_core.tools import tool, StructuredTool
from langchain.agents.middleware.human_in_the_loop import ApproveDecision, 
RejectDecision, RespondDecision
@tool
def search(query: str, limit: int = 5) -> str:
    """Search the web."""
    return query
schema = search.tool_call_schema
print(schema, schema.__name__, schema.__doc__)
def keep(v, handler):
    handler(v)
    return v
Args = Annotated[schema, WrapValidator(keep)]
EditedAction = TypedDict("EditedAction", {"name": Literal["search"], "args": 
Args})
EditDecision = TypedDict("EditDecision", {"type": Literal["edit"], 
"edited_action": EditedAction})
U = Annotated[Union[ApproveDecision, EditDecision, RejectDecision, 
RespondDecision], Field(discriminator="type")]
ta = TypeAdapter(U)
print(json.dumps(ta.json_schema(), indent=1))
for d in [{"type": "edit"}, {"type": "nope"}, 
{"type":"edit","edited_action":{"name":"x","args":{"query":"a"}}},
          {"type":"edit","edited_action":{"name":"search","args":{}}},
          {"type":"edit","edited_action":{"name":"search","args":{"query":"a","e
xtra":1}}}]:
    try: print("OK", ta.validate_python(d))
    except ValidationError as e: print(e.error_count(), [(x["loc"], x["msg"]) 
for x in e.errors()])
EOF

Reasoning:
For a simple Tool, tool_call_schema is a model that requires `config`, but the 
model actually sees `__arg1`, so validating against it would wrongly reject 
valid calls. The schema the model sees is what convert_to_openai_function 
produces, so I should check whether the tool is simple before deciding which 
schema to validate against.

I'm working out how to pick the validation path: a simple tool's 
tool_call_schema is a model with `config` that the model never sees, so I need 
to mirror langchain_core's check of whether the tool is simple before choosing 
which schema to validate against. The validated path should apply only when the 
tool's schema is a Pydantic BaseModel and the tool isn't a simple one; otherwise
I'll display the parameters from convert_to_openai_function.

I'm settling on the permissive display: dereferenced 
`convert_to_openai_function` parameters under the tool name, with no local refs 
to break nesting. To get that into `$defs` through Pydantic, I'm thinking of a 
model with `extra="allow"` and no fields, then overwriting its schema via 
`json_schema_extra`. Validation would wrap that same extra-allow model, so the 
accepted arguments stay unrestricted.

Now I'm weighing whether a dynamic Pydantic model per call is too slow, and 
considering caching, though tools may not be hashable.

I'm considering how to name the permissive definition after the tool itself. If 
the validated path uses a schema that's already named after the tool, it works, 
but a custom schema could carry a different name, so I'd want to wrap it in a 
subclass named for the tool with the tool's description.

I'm checking whether a subclass is needed to keep the permissive definition 
named after the tool, and I'm leaning toward only subclassing when the schema's 
name or description doesn't already match the tool's.

I'm checking whether the `json_schema()` call on the TypeAdapter will behave the
same way for the permissive model, since that's the last open question about the
display path.

I'm settling the single-decision case: it returns that decision's plain object 
schema directly, while a lone "edit" returns the dynamic EditDecision with its 
$defs for EditedAction and the tool. Unknown names in allowed_decisions will 
raise a ValueError inside _decision_schema. I'm also noting that a nested model 
sharing a name with one of my definitions would get disambiguated by Pydantic, 
which changes the $defs keys, but I'm treating that as rare enough to accept.

I'm laying out the middleware's components now, starting with the interrupt mode
type and a `ToolApprovalRequest` for reviewing one tool call before it runs.

        decisions: list[Decision]

        decisions: list[Decision]
        """The decisions made by the human."""
    
    
    class ToolApprovalRequest(TypedDict):
        """Request for human review of a single tool call, made just before it 
runs."""
    
        type: Literal["tool_approval"]
        """Marks the request as the review of one tool call."""
    
        tool_call_id: str
        """ID of the tool call under review."""
    
        name: str
        """Name of the tool being called."""
    
        args: dict[str, Any]
        """Arguments the model called the tool with."""
    
        description: str
        """The description of the tool call to be reviewed."""
    
    
    def _keep_reviewer_args(args: Any, handler: ValidatorFunctionWrapHandler) ->
Any:
        """Validate the reviewer's args, keeping them as given so the tool 
receives them."""
        handler(args)
        return args
    
    
    def _edited_args_type(tool_name: str, tool: BaseTool | None) -> Any:
        """Type of the args a reviewer may give `tool`, named after the tool.
    
        When the model sees the tool's arguments as a pydantic model, the args 
are
        validated against it. Otherwise the arguments the model sees are shown, 
if there is
        a tool, and any object is accepted.
        """
        schema = tool.tool_call_schema if tool is not None else None
        sees_schema = not (isinstance(tool, Tool) and not tool.args_schema)
        if sees_schema and isinstance(schema, type) and issubclass(schema, 
BaseModel):
            return Annotated[schema, WrapValidator(_keep_reviewer_args)]
    
        shown: dict[str, Any] = {"title": tool_name, "type": "object"}
        if tool is not None:
            shown.update(convert_to_openai_function(tool)["parameters"])
            if tool.description:
                shown["description"] = tool.description
    
        def show(json_schema: dict[str, Any], _: type[BaseModel]) -> None:
            json_schema.clear()
            json_schema.update(shown)
    
        any_object = create_model(
            tool_name, __config__=ConfigDict(extra="allow", 
json_schema_extra=show)
        )
        return Annotated[any_object, WrapValidator(_keep_reviewer_args)]
    
    
    def _decision_schema(
        allowed_decisions: Sequence[DecisionType],
        tool_name: str,
        tool: BaseTool | None = None,
    ) -> Any:
        """Type of the answers a reviewer may give for a call to `tool_name`.
    
        Each allowed decision is one member of a union tagged by `type`, in
        `allowed_decisions` order. A single allowed decision is its own type. An
`edit`
        must name `tool_name`, and its args must fit the tool's arguments as the
model sees
        them; the args it carries are the reviewer's own, not the validated 
ones.
    
        Args:
            allowed_decisions: The decisions the reviewer may make.
            tool_name: Name of the tool being called.
            tool: The tool, whose arguments an `edit` is validated against.
    
        Returns:
            A type pydantic's `TypeAdapter` accepts.
    
        Raises:
            ValueError: If `allowed_decisions` is empty or names an unknown 
decision.
        """
        edited_action = TypedDict(  # type: ignore[operator]
            "EditedAction",
            {"name": Literal[tool_name], "args": _edited_args_type(tool_name, 
tool)},
        )
        edited_action.__doc__ = Action.__doc__
        edit_decision = TypedDict(  # type: ignore[operator]
            "EditDecision", {"type": Literal["edit"], "edited_action": 
edited_action}
        )
        edit_decision.__doc__ = EditDecision.__doc__
        decision_types: dict[str, Any] = {
            "approve": ApproveDecision,
            "edit": edit_decision,
            "reject": RejectDecision,
            "respond": RespondDecision,
        }
        allowed = list(dict.fromkeys(allowed_decisions))
        if not allowed or (unknown := [d for d in allowed if d not in 
decision_types]):
            msg = (
                f"`allowed_decisions` for tool '{tool_name}' must be a non-empty
list of "
                f"{list(decision_types)}, got {list(allowed_decisions)!r}."
            )
            raise ValueError(msg)
        members = tuple(decision_types[d] for d in allowed)
        if len(members) == 1:
            return members[0]
        return Annotated[Union[members], Field(discriminator="type")]  # noqa: 
UP007

+171|
+172|    tool_call_id: str
+173|    """ID of the tool call under review."""
+174|
+175|    name: str
+176|    """Name of the tool being called."""
+177|
+178|    args: dict[str, Any]
+179|    """Arguments the model called the tool with."""
+180|
+181|    description: str
+182|    """The description of the tool call to be reviewed."""
+183|
+184|
+185|def _keep_reviewer_args(args: Any, handler: ValidatorFunctionWrapHandler) 
-> Any:
+186|    """Validate the reviewer's args, keeping them as given so the tool 
receives them."""
+187|    handler(args)
+188|    return args
+189|
+190|
+191|def _edited_args_type(tool_name: str, tool: BaseTool | None) -> Any:
+192|    """Type of the args a reviewer may give `tool`, named after the tool.
+193|
+194|    When the model sees the tool's arguments as a pydantic model, the args 
are
+195|    validated against it. Otherwise the arguments the model sees are shown,
if there is
+196|    a tool, and any object is accepted.
+197|    """
+198|    schema = tool.tool_call_schema if tool is not None else None
+199|    sees_schema = not (isinstance(tool, Tool) and not tool.args_schema)
+200|    if sees_schema and isinstance(schema, type) and issubclass(schema, 
BaseModel):
+201|        return Annotated[schema, WrapValidator(_keep_reviewer_args)]
+202|
+203|    shown: dict[str, Any] = {"title": tool_name, "type": "object"}
+204|    if tool is not None:
+205|        shown.update(convert_to_openai_function(tool)["parameters"])
+206|        if tool.description:
+207|            shown["description"] = tool.description
+208|
+209|    def show(json_schema: dict[str, Any], _: type[BaseModel]) -> None:
+210|        json_schema.clear()
+211|        json_schema.update(shown)
+212|
+213|    any_object = create_model(
+214|        tool_name, __config__=ConfigDict(extra="allow", 
json_schema_extra=show)
+215|    )
+216|    return Annotated[any_object, WrapValidator(_keep_reviewer_args)]
+217|
+218|
+219|def _decision_schema(
+220|    allowed_decisions: Sequence[DecisionType],
+221|    tool_name: str,
+222|    tool: BaseTool | None = None,
+223|) -> Any:
+224|    """Type of the answers a reviewer may give for a call to `tool_name`.
+225|
+226|    Each allowed decision is one member of a union tagged by `type`, in
+227|    `allowed_decisions` order. A single allowed decision is its own type. 
An `edit`
+228|    must name `tool_name`, and its args must fit the tool's arguments as 
the model sees
+229|    them; the args it carries are the reviewer's own, not the validated 
ones.
+230|
+231|    Args:
+232|        allowed_decisions: The decisions the reviewer may make.
+233|        tool_name: Name of the tool being called.
+234|        tool: The tool, whose arguments an `edit` is validated against.
+235|
+236|    Returns:
+237|        A type pydantic's `TypeAdapter` accepts.
+238|
+239|    Raises:
+240|        ValueError: If `allowed_decisions` is empty or names an unknown 
decision.
+241|    """
+242|    edited_action = TypedDict(  # type: ignore[operator]
+243|        "EditedAction",
+244|        {"name": Literal[tool_name], "args": _edited_args_type(tool_name, 
tool)},
+245|    )
+246|    edited_action.__doc__ = Action.__doc__
+247|    edit_decision = TypedDict(  # type: ignore[operator]
+248|        "EditDecision", {"type": Literal["edit"], "edited_action": 
edited_action}
+249|    )
+250|    edit_decision.__doc__ = EditDecision.__doc__
+251|    decision_types: dict[str, Any] = {
+252|        "approve": ApproveDecision,
+253|        "edit": edit_decision,
+254|        "reject": RejectDecision,
+255|        "respond": RespondDecision,
+256|    }
+257|    allowed = list(dict.fromkeys(allowed_decisions))
+258|    if not allowed or (unknown := [d for d in allowed if d not in 
decision_types]):
+259|        msg = (
+260|            f"`allowed_decisions` for tool '{tool_name}' must be a 
non-empty list of "
+261|            f"{list(decision_types)}, got {list(allowed_decisions)!r}."
+262|        )
+263|        raise ValueError(msg)
+264|    members = tuple(decision_types[d] for d in allowed)
+265|    if len(members) == 1:
+266|        return members[0]
+267|    return Annotated[Union[members], Field(discriminator="type")]  # noqa: 
UP007

+343|    `context`, `store`, `stream_writer`, `execution_info`, and 
`server_info` from the
+344|    node-level `Runtime`, while `tool_call_id` is populated from the 
current tool
+345|    call. The `tools` argument is not supplied, so it uses its default 
empty list.
+346|
+347|    In `'per_call'` mode, the predicate is called just before the tool call
runs, with
+348|    the request that is about to run it.
 349|
 350|    Example:
[end of edit 1 / 1]

                    `pydantic.ValidationError` and is not kept, so the pause can
be
                    answered again. List this middleware before middleware that
                    retries tool calls, so a retried call is not reviewed again.
            description_prefix:''')

p = 
"/app/langchain/libs/langchain_v1/langchain/agents/middleware/human_in_the_loop.
py"
s = open(p).read()
def rep(old, new):
    global s
    assert s.count(old) == 1, old
    s = s.replace(old, new)

rep('''        """Trigger interrupt flows for relevant tool calls after an 
`AIMessage`.

        Args:
            state: The current agent state.
            runtime: The runtime context.

        Returns:
            Updated message with the revised tool calls.

        Raises:
            ValueError: If the number of human decisions does not match the 
number of
                interrupted tool calls.
        """
        messages = state["messages"]
        if not messages:
            return None
''', '''        """Trigger interrupt flows for relevant tool calls after an 
`AIMessage`.

        In `'per_call'` mode, tool calls are reviewed as they run instead, so 
this
        does nothing.

        Args:
            state: The current agent state.
            runtime: The runtime context.

        Returns:
            Updated message with the revised tool calls.

        Raises:
            ValueError: If the number of human decisions does not match the 
number of
                interrupted tool calls.
        """
        if self.interrupt_mode == "per_call":
            return None

        messages = state["messages"]
        if not messages:
            return None
''')

rep('''    def _reviewer_edit(self, request: ToolCallRequest) -> Action | 
None:''',
'''    def _review(self, request: ToolCallRequest) -> tuple[Action | None, 
ToolMessage | None]:
        """Pause for a reviewer's decision on the call about to run, if it needs
one.

        Returns:
            The reviewer's replacement for the call, if they edited it, and the 
tool
                message to return without running the call, if they rejected it 
or
                responded on behalf of the tool.

        Raises:
            ValueError: If the call has no ID.
        """
        tool_call = request.tool_call
        config = self.interrupt_on.get(tool_call["name"])
        if config is None:
            return None, None
        when = config.get("when")
        if when is not None and not when(request):
            return None, None
        if tool_call.get("id") is None:
            msg = (
                f"Tool call `{tool_call['name']}` has no ID, so it cannot be 
reviewed "
                "on its own and matched with its result."
            )
            raise ValueError(msg)

        action_request, _ = self._create_action_and_config(
            tool_call, config, request.state, get_runtime()
        )
        approval_request = ToolApprovalRequest(
            type="tool_approval",
            tool_call_id=cast("str", tool_call["id"]),
            name=tool_call["name"],
            args=tool_call["args"],
            description=action_request["description"],
        )
        decision: Decision = interrupt(
            approval_request,
            response_schema=_decision_schema(
                config["allowed_decisions"], tool_call["name"], request.tool
            ),
        )
        _, tool_message = self._process_decision(decision, tool_call, config)
        if decision["type"] == "edit":
            return decision["edited_action"], tool_message

'''    def _review(self, request: ToolCallRequest) -> tuple[Action | None, 
ToolMessage | None]:
        """Pause for a reviewer's decision on the call about to run, if it needs
one.
n        Returns:
            The reviewer's replacement for the call, if they edited it, and the 
tool
                message to return without running the call, if they rejected it 
or
                responded on behalf of the tool.
        Raises:
            ValueError: If the call has no ID.
        """
        tool_call = request.tool_call
        config = self.interrupt_on.get(tool_call["name"])
        if config is None:
            return None, None
        when = config.get("when")
        if when is not None and not when(request):
            return None, None
        if tool_call.get("id") is None:
            msg = (
                f"Tool call `{tool_call['name']}` has no ID, so it cannot be 
reviewed "
                "on its own and matched with its result."
            )
            raise ValueError(msg)
        action_request, _ = self._create_action_and_config(
            tool_call, config, request.state, get_runtime()
        )
        approval_request = ToolApprovalRequest(
            type="tool_approval",
            tool_call_id=cast("str", tool_call["id"]),
            name=tool_call["name"],
            args=tool_call["args"],
            description=action_request["description"],
        )
        decision: Decision = interrupt(
            approval_request,
            response_schema=_decision_schema(
                config["allowed_decisions"], tool_call["name"], request.tool
            ),
        )
        _, tool_message = self._process_decision(decision, tool_call, config)
        if decision["type"] == "edit":
            return decision["edited_action"], tool_message
        return None, tool_message

def test_human_in_the_loop_middleware_edit_executes_reviewers_call() -> None:
    """The reviewer's args run while the model's own call stays in the 
message."""
    executed: list[dict[str, Any]] = []

    @tool
    def write_file_tool(path: str, content: str) -> str:
        """Write content to a file."""
        executed.append({"path": path, "content": content})
        return f"File written to {path}"

    model = FakeToolCallingModel(
        tool_calls=[
            [
                ToolCall(
                    name="write_file_tool", args={"path": "notes.txt", 
"content": "mine"}, id="1"
                )
            ],
            [],
        ]
    )
    agent = create_agent(
        model=model,
        tools=[write_file_tool],
        middleware=[
            HumanInTheLoopMiddleware(
                interrupt_on={"write_file_tool": {"allowed_decisions": 
["approve", "edit"]}}
            )
        ],
        checkpointer=InMemorySaver(),
    )
    config: RunnableConfig = {"configurable": {"thread_id": 
"executes-reviewer-call"}}
    agent.invoke({"messages": [HumanMessage("write it")]}, config)
    final = agent.invoke(
        Command(
            resume={
                "decisions": [
                    {
                        "type": "edit",
                        "edited_action": {
                            "name": "write_file_tool",
                            "args": {"path": "notes.txt", "content": 
"reviewers"},
                        },
                    }
                ]
            }
        ),
        config,
    )

    assert executed == [{"path": "notes.txt", "content": "reviewers"}]
    ai_message = next(m for m in final["messages"] if isinstance(m, AIMessage) 
and m.tool_calls)
    assert ai_message.tool_calls[0]["args"] == {"path": "notes.txt", "content": 
"mine"}
    tool_message = next(m for m in final["messages"] if isinstance(m, 
ToolMessage))
    assert "reviewers" in tool_message.content
    _assert_tool_messages_are_paired(final["messages"])

import asyncio, sys
from pydantic import ValidationError
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, ToolCall, ToolMessage
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
from langgraph.types import Command
from langchain.agents import create_agent
from langchain.agents.middleware.human_in_the_loop import 
HumanInTheLoopMiddleware
sys.path.insert(0, ".")
from tests.unit_tests.agents.model import FakeToolCallingModel

events = []
@tool
async def search(query: str) -> str:
    """Search the web."""
    events.append(("search-start", query))
    await asyncio.sleep(0.3)
    events.append(("search-done", query))
    return f"results for {query}"

@tool
async def delete(path: str) -> str:
    """Delete a file."""
    events.append(("delete", path))
    return f"deleted {path}"

async def main():
    model = FakeToolCallingModel(tool_calls=[[ToolCall(name="search", 
args={"query": "cats"}, id="c1"),
                                              ToolCall(name="delete", 
args={"path": "/tmp/x"}, id="c2")], []])
    agent = create_agent(model=model, tools=[search, delete], 
checkpointer=InMemorySaver(),
        middleware=[HumanInTheLoopMiddleware({"search": True, "delete": 
{"allowed_decisions": ["approve", "reject"]}}, interrupt_mode="per_call")])
    cfg = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "t"}}
    r = await agent.ainvoke({"messages": [HumanMessage("go")]}, cfg)
    ints = {i.value["name"]: i for i in r["__interrupt__"]}
    try:
        await agent.ainvoke(Command(resume={ints["search"].id: {"type": 
"approve"}, ints["delete"].id: {"type": "edit"}}), cfg)
    except ValidationError as e:
        print("VALIDATION", e.error_count())
    print("events", events)
    await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
    r = await agent.ainvoke(Command(resume={ints["delete"].id: {"type": 
"approve"}}), cfg)
    print("events", events)
    print("final interrupts", r.get("__interrupt__"))
    for m in r["messages"]:
        if isinstance(m, ToolMessage): print("TM", m.tool_call_id, m.status, 
m.content[:80])
asyncio.run(main())

Summary: terminal: {"command": "cd 
/app/langgraph/libs/langgraph/langgraph/pregel; grep -n 
\"SKIP_RERAISE_SET\\|_handled_exception_ids\\|_should_stop_others\\|_panic_or_pr
oceed\" _runner.py | head -30; grep -n \"async def atick\" -A100 _runner.py | 
grep -n \"wait\\|stop\\|panic\\|cancel\\|break\\|reraise\" | head -30"}

Reasoning:
The search tool had already started when delete's validation failed and 
cancelled the run. On resume, search ran again without pausing for approval, 
which makes me suspect its earlier "approve" answer was kept and sent into the 
task's writes before the cancellation.

I'm checking the sync-versus-async difference: in async, cancelling the sibling 
task interrupts the tool mid-run, and the persisted approval gets reused on 
resume, so the tool runs again. The real issue is that one invalid answer 
cancels siblings that have already accepted theirs, and I'm weighing whether to 
validate all answers before any task runs tools or to stop a validation failure 
from cancelling its siblings.

I'm considering whether a rejected answer should count as a failure that cancels
siblings at all. If it behaved more like a pending interrupt, the other tasks 
could finish, and the validation error could be raised once everything is done.

I'm weighing whether a rejected answer should cancel its siblings at all, or be 
held back so the other tasks finish and the validation error is raised 
afterward. The awkward part is that a pydantic ValidationError can't be 
subclassed or tagged easily, so the runner would have to track that exception 
some other way.

I'm checking whether the sync executor waits for running futures on exit, since 
that would explain why the search finished before the invoke call returned.

I'm weighing whether every answer should be validated before any task runs a 
tool, so a bad answer for one call can't cancel a sibling that is already 
executing.

I'm considering having the runner let a rejected answer's sibling tasks run to 
completion instead of cancelling them, then raise the validation error 
afterward. That would make the async path behave like sync. The open question is
how interrupt() could mark the error so the runner knows not to stop the others.

I'm considering a wrapper exception that subclasses GraphBubbleUp, so that a 
rejected answer doesn't stop sibling tasks and the original ValidationError is 
unwrapped at the top level.

I'm still weighing whether interrupt should raise a plain pydantic 
ValidationError out of invoke, since the spec seems to want exactly that, and 
that settles whether the runner can wrap it without changing what the caller 
sees.

I'm checking how the runner could tell a rejected answer apart from other 
ValidationErrors, since treating every pydantic error as non-cancelling would 
change behaviour for unrelated nodes. One idea is to have interrupt() record the
exception in a marker the runner reads, similar to the handled-exception ids it 
already uses for error routing. The open question is how interrupt() gets access
to that runner state through the config.

I'm wondering whether the sibling tasks should keep running after a rejected 
answer, since the sync path lets them finish while the async path cancels them 
mid-tool. Cancelling a task that already had side effects is what lets its 
approval get reused on resume and the tool run twice, so a general cancellation 
rule looks like the wrong fix.

I'm considering having the runner let sibling tasks finish when one answer is 
rejected, rather than cancelling them mid-tool, since that would make the async 
path match the sync one. The open question is how the runner could tell a 
rejected answer apart from other validation errors without changing behaviour 
for unrelated nodes.

I'm considering a few ways to flag a rejected answer so the runner can tell it 
apart from other errors, such as tagging the exception or recording it in a 
registry the runner already uses, but each has its own drawbacks.

I'm testing whether a ValidationError can carry an extra attribute so the runner
can spot a rejected answer, and I'm checking the Python 3.10 floor, since 
add_note only exists from 3.11.

I'm settling on a cleaner model: a rejected answer would leave its interrupt 
pending, letting sibling tasks finish, and then the run would raise the 
ValidationError instead of returning the interrupt.

I'm considering a GraphBubbleUp subclass for the rejected-answer error, since 
control-flow exceptions get passed through by middleware and retry logic. That 
would let the bad answer fail loudly without being swallowed along the way.

I'm wondering whether a plain ValidationError would get swallowed by a ToolNode 
set to handle tool errors, which would turn it into a ToolMessage and end the 
pause. Making it a GraphBubbleUp subclass would keep it out of that error 
handling.

I'm weighing whether a validation error should be wrapped in a control-flow 
exception, so standard handlers can't swallow it, then re-raised as a plain 
ValidationError at the invoke boundary. The open question is whether user code 
that wraps interrupt() in a try block would need to catch it.

I'm checking whether pydantic's ValidationError can be subclassed alongside 
GraphBubbleUp, since that would let the rejected-answer error skip standard 
error handlers while still surfacing as a ValidationError at the invoke 
boundary.

I'm considering whether a plain ValidationError would get swallowed by error 
handling, which is why the "listed after" ordering matters. Since the runner 
can't stop sibling tasks for that case anyway, the real remaining problem is the
async cancellation, so I'm going to test whether a ValidationError can take 
extra attributes or notes, which would let the runner recognize a rejected 
answer.

Rejecting one answer makes the async runner cancel a sibling task that had 
already been approved, so `search` runs twice on resume; the sync path avoids 
this only because worker threads can't be cancelled. I'm checking how the runner
handles parallel task errors before picking a fix.

def create_error_message(*, message: str, error_code: ErrorCode) -> str:
    return (
        f"{message}\n"
        "For troubleshooting, visit: 
https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/"
        f"errors/{error_code.value}"
    )


class GraphBubbleUp(Exception):
    pass


class GraphDrained(GraphBubbleUp):
    """Raised when a graph run exits early due to a drain request.

    This indicates the graph stopped cooperatively at a superstep boundary
    because `RunControl.request_drain()` was called (e.g., in response to
    SIGTERM). The checkpoint is saved and the run can be resumed later.
    """

    def __init__(self, reason: str = "shutdown") -> None:
        self.reason = reason
        super().__init__(f"Graph drained: {reason}")


class GraphRecursionError(RecursionError):
    """Raised when the graph has exhausted the maximum number of steps.

    This prevents infinite loops. To increase the maximum number of steps,
    run your graph with a config specifying a higher `recursion_limit`.

    Troubleshooting guides:

    - 
[`GRAPH_RECURSION_LIMIT`](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/GRAPH_
RECURSION_LIMIT)

    Examples:

        graph = builder.compile()
        graph.invoke(
            {"messages": [("user", "Hello, world!")]},
            # The config is the second positional argument
            {"recursion_limit": 1000},
        )
    """

    pass


class InvalidUpdateError(Exception):
    """Raised when attempting to update a channel with an invalid set of 
updates.

    Troubleshooting guides:

    - 
[`INVALID_CONCURRENT_GRAPH_UPDATE`](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgr
aph/INVALID_CONCURRENT_GRAPH_UPDATE)
    - 
[`INVALID_GRAPH_NODE_RETURN_VALUE`](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgr
aph/INVALID_GRAPH_NODE_RETURN_VALUE)
    """

    pass


class GraphInterrupt(GraphBubbleUp):
    """Raised when a subgraph is interrupted, suppressed by the root graph.
    Never raised directly, or surfaced to the user."""

    def __init__(self, interrupts: Sequence[Interrupt] = ()) -> None:
        super().__init__(interrupts)


@deprecated(
    "NodeInterrupt is deprecated. Please use 
[`interrupt`][langgraph.types.interrupt] instead.",
    category=None,
)
class NodeInterrupt(GraphInterrupt):
    """Raised by a node to interrupt execution."""

    def _suppress_interrupt(
        self,
        exc_type: type[BaseException] | None,
        exc_value: BaseException | None,
        traceback: TracebackType | None,
    ) -> bool | None:
        # persist current checkpoint and writes
        if self.durability == "exit" and (
            # if it's a top graph
            not self.is_nested
            # or a nested graph with error or interrupt
            or exc_value is not None
            # or a nested graph with checkpointer=True
            or all(NS_END not in part for part in self.checkpoint_ns)
        ):
            self._put_exit_delta_writes()
            self._put_checkpoint(self.checkpoint_metadata)
            self._put_pending_writes()
        # suppress interrupt
        if isinstance(exc_value, GraphInterrupt) and not self.is_nested:
            interrupt = exc_value
            interrupts = tuple(interrupt.args[0]) if interrupt.args else ()
            self._push_graph_lifecycle_event("interrupt", interrupts=interrupts)
            # emit one last "values" event, with pending writes applied
            if (
                hasattr(self, "tasks")
                and self.checkpoint_pending_writes
                and any(task.writes for task in self.tasks.values())
            ):
                updated_channels = apply_writes(
                    self.checkpoint,
                    self.channels,
                    self.tasks.values(),
                    self.checkpointer_get_next_version,
                    self.trigger_to_nodes,
                )
                if not updated_channels.isdisjoint(
                    (self.output_keys,)
                    if isinstance(self.output_keys, str)
                    else self.output_keys
                ):
                    self._emit(
                        "values",
                        map_output_values,
                        self.output_keys,
                        [w for t in self.tasks.values() for w in t.writes],
                        self.channels,
                    )
            # emit INTERRUPT if exception is empty (otherwise emitted by 
put_writes)
            if not interrupt.args or not interrupt.args[0]:
                interrupt_payload = interrupt.args[0] if interrupt.args else ()
                self._emit(
                    "updates",
                    lambda: iter([{INTERRUPT: interrupt_payload}]),
                )
            # save final output
            self.output = read_channels(self.channels, self.output_keys)
            # suppress interrupt
            return True
        elif exc_type is None:
            # save final output
            self.output = read_channels(self.channels, self.output_keys)

    def _emit(
        self,
        mode: StreamMode,
        values: Callable[P, Iterator[Any]],
        *args: P.args,
        **kwargs: P.kwargs,
    ) -> None:
        if self.stream is None:
            return
        debug_remap = mode in ("checkpoints", "tasks") and "debug" in 
self.stream.modes
        if mode not in self.stream.modes and not debug_remap:
            return
        for v in values(*args, **kwargs):
            if mode in self.stream.modes:
                self.stream((self.checkpoint_ns, mode, v))
            # "debug" mode is "checkpoints" or "tasks" with a wrapper dict
            if debug_remap:

                    (

def _status_from_exception(err: BaseException) -> tuple[SubgraphStatus, str | 
None]:
    """Map a run exception to a subgraph terminal status and error string."""
    if isinstance(err, GraphDrained):
        return "drained", None
    if isinstance(err, GraphInterrupt):
        return "interrupted", None
    return "failed", str(err)

    a populated `interrupts` list, not as `error`.

class GraphInterrupt(GraphBubbleUp):
    """Raised when a subgraph is interrupted, suppressed by the root graph.
    Never raised directly, or surfaced to the user."""

    def __init__(self, interrupts: Sequence[Interrupt] = ()) -> None:
        super().__init__(interrupts)


@deprecated(
    "NodeInterrupt is deprecated. Please use 
[`interrupt`][langgraph.types.interrupt] instead.",
    category=None,
)
class NodeInterrupt(GraphInterrupt):
    """Raised by a node to interrupt execution."""

    def __init__(self, value: Any, id: str | None = None) -> None:
        warn(
            "NodeInterrupt is deprecated. Please use `langgraph.types.interrupt`
instead.",
            LangGraphDeprecatedSinceV10,
            stacklevel=2,
        )
        if id is None:
            super().__init__([Interrupt(value=value)])
        else:
            super().__init__([Interrupt(value=value, id=id)])


class ParentCommand(GraphBubbleUp):
    args: tuple[Command]

    def __init__(self, command: Command) -> None:
        super().__init__(command)

    # track interrupt index
    scratchpad = conf[CONFIG_KEY_SCRATCHPAD]
    idx = scratchpad.interrupt_counter()
    adapter = (
        None
        if response_schema is None or isinstance(response_schema, dict)
        else TypeAdapter(response_schema)
    )
    pending = Interrupt.from_ns(
        value=value,
        ns=conf[CONFIG_KEY_CHECKPOINT_NS],
        response_schema=adapter.json_schema() if adapter else response_schema,
    )
    # find previous resume values
    if idx < len(scratchpad.resume):
        v = scratchpad.resume[idx]
    # find current resume value
    elif idx == len(scratchpad.resume) and scratchpad.mapped_resume:
        v = scratchpad.mapped_resume.pop()
    elif (v := scratchpad.get_null_resume(True)) is not None:
        assert len(scratchpad.resume) == idx, (scratchpad.resume, idx)
    else:
        # no resume value found
        raise GraphInterrupt((pending,))
    # validate before keeping, so a rejected answer leaves its interrupt pending
    try:
        answer = v if adapter is None else adapter.validate_python(v)
    except ValidationError as exc:
        if idx < len(scratchpad.resume):
            # answers match interrupts by position, so the ones after it go too
            del scratchpad.resume[idx:]
            conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
        raise ResumeRejected((pending,), exc) from exc
    if idx == len(scratchpad.resume):
        scratchpad.resume.append(v)
    conf[CONFIG_KEY_SEND]([(RESUME, scratchpad.resume)])
    return answer

I'm noticing that `pending` gets built on every call, including 
`adapter.json_schema()`, even when resuming doesn't need it. Since schema 
generation costs something each time, I'd rather defer it, but `ResumeRejected` 
still needs it, so I'm thinking about a small nested helper that computes it 
only in the failure and no-answer branches.

Now I'm weighing whether to build the nested helper or a module-level one, and 
I'm leaning toward a nested function since langgraph code already uses them. 
There's also a concern that `adapter.json_schema()` could raise on arbitrary 
types, which would break resume even when validation would have worked, so 
computing it lazily avoids that.

from pydantic import ValidationError
from typing_extensions import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
from langgraph.func import entrypoint, task
from langgraph.types import interrupt, Command

class S(TypedDict):
    x: int

def inner(s):
    return {"x": interrupt("age?", response_schema=int)}
sub = StateGraph(S); sub.add_node("inner", inner); sub.add_edge(START, "inner");
sub = sub.compile()
g = StateGraph(S); g.add_node("outer", sub); g.add_edge(START, "outer")
app = g.compile(checkpointer=InMemorySaver())
cfg = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "1"}}
r = app.invoke({"x": 0}, cfg); i = r["__interrupt__"][0]; print(i)
try: app.invoke(Command(resume={i.id: "old"}), cfg)
except ValidationError as e: print("VALIDATION", e.errors()[0]["msg"])
print("state interrupts", app.get_state(cfg, subgraphs=True).interrupts)
print(app.invoke(Command(resume={i.id: "41"}), cfg))

@task
def ask(q: str) -> int:
    return interrupt(q, response_schema=int)

@entrypoint(checkpointer=InMemorySaver())
def flow(q: str) -> int:
    return ask(q).result() + 1

cfg = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "2"}}
r = flow.invoke("n?", cfg); i = r if not isinstance(r, dict) else r
print(list(flow.stream("n?", {"configurable": {"thread_id": "3"}})))
cfg = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "3"}}
iid = flow.get_state(cfg).interrupts[0].id
try: flow.invoke(Command(resume={iid: "x"}), cfg)
except ValidationError as e: print("VALIDATION", e.errors()[0]["msg"])
print(flow.invoke(Command(resume={iid: "1"}), cfg))
EOF

import pytest
from langgraph.checkpoint.base import BaseCheckpointSaver
from typing_extensions import TypedDict

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph
from langgraph.types import Durability

pytestmark = pytest.mark.anyio


def test_interruption_without_state_updates(
    sync_checkpointer: BaseCheckpointSaver, durability: Durability
) -> None:
    """Test interruption without state updates. This test confirms that
    interrupting doesn't require a state key having been updated in the prev 
step"""

    class State(TypedDict):
        input: str

    def noop(_state):
        pass

    builder = StateGraph(State)
    builder.add_node("step_1", noop)
    builder.add_node("step_2", noop)
    builder.add_node("step_3", noop)
    builder.add_edge(START, "step_1")
    builder.add_edge("step_1", "step_2")
    builder.add_edge("step_2", "step_3")
    builder.add_edge("step_3", END)

    graph = builder.compile(checkpointer=sync_checkpointer, interrupt_after="*")

    initial_input = {"input": "hello world"}
    thread = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "1"}}

    graph.invoke(initial_input, thread, durability=durability)
    assert graph.get_state(thread).next == ("step_2",)
    n_checkpoints = len([c for c in graph.get_state_history(thread)])
    assert n_checkpoints == (3 if durability != "exit" else 1)

    graph.invoke(None, thread, durability=durability)
    assert graph.get_state(thread).next == ("step_3",)
    n_checkpoints = len([c for c in graph.get_state_history(thread)])
    assert n_checkpoints == (4 if durability != "exit" else 2)

    graph.invoke(None, thread, durability=durability)
    assert graph.get_state(thread).next == ()
    n_checkpoints = len([c for c in graph.get_state_history(thread)])
    assert n_checkpoints == (5 if durability != "exit" else 3)
